## 1. What this report holds and how to read it

A collection: the member words, the Strong's codes behind them, and what the study has recorded for the occurrences of those words, from the base data through the observations and the claims into the narrative. Sections 2 and 3 say what the data is; 4 and 5 say what the study has recorded; 6 lists the phrases; 7 lists the faults found in the stored rows. Verses are in Scripture order (book in cfg_book_order, chapter, verse). The detail that does not fit a reading (every Strong's entry in full, every observation and node, the change history, the narrative text) is in the workbook, one sheet for each listing; the sheet is named where it is cited.

### 3.4 H7451B רַע ra "bad: evil": 89 own occurrences in 86 verses

- **Meaning as stored:** Hebrew; native word רַע, transliteration ra; STEP gloss "bad: evil"; sense head "bad: evil" (the code is its own lemma).
- **Senses (first level of the meaning tree of the lemma H7451):** evil, misery, distress, injury.
- **Related terms (8):** H4827 me.ra "mischief"; H7451A ra "bad: harmful"; H7451C ra.ah "distress: harm"; H7451H ra "bad: evil"; H7451I ra.ah "distress: evil"; H7455 ro.a "evil"; more in the workbook.
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M58 Wickedness (medium).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 116; verses STEP attests 110; verse_lexical rows the parse found 113.

- **Carried by the member words:** evil (83), wicked (3), malice (2), evil man (1).

## 4. What the study has recorded for these occurrences

Of 1106 occurrences, 294 have an engagement observation and 812 have none recorded. There are 473 live observations on them (3 engagement, 388 of other recorded stages, 82 of the retired catalogue stages) and 14 withdrawn.

### 4.1 Engagement: what each word does for the inner being (3 observations)

An engagement observation states, for the surface in a verse, whether it voices an inner-being operation (use, face) or is silent. Each is one statement applied to every occurrence it covers.

- **Observation 21505** (engagement, tag face, status draft): Wicked is here the wickedness, ungodliness or sinfulness of a person as a state or practice of the heart, set against godliness and what is right (ungodliness, a heart busy with iniquity, a wicked deed, sinful passions).
  - applies to 37 occurrence(s): Gen 13:13 (wicked), Gen 38:7 (wicked), Gen 38:10 (wicked), Num 14:27 (wicked), Num 14:35 (wicked), Est 7:6 (wicked), Psa 10:3 (wicked), Psa 10:4 (wicked), Psa 10:13 (wicked), Psa 36:1 (wicked), Psa 37:21 (wicked), Psa 37:32 (wicked); and 25 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 21504** (engagement, tag qualification, status draft): Wicked gives a moral condition to what it binds (an upright heart, a righteous or wicked way, godly grief, walking uprightly, a sinful nation): the uprightness, godliness or wickedness of the heart, speech, way or conduct.
  - applies to 18 occurrence(s): 2Ki 17:11 (wicked), 2Ch 7:14 (wicked), Ezr 4:12 (wicked), Neh 9:35 (wicked), Psa 94:20 (wicked), Psa 141:4 (wicked), Pro 6:18 (wicked), Pro 17:4 (wicked), Pro 28:15 (wicked), Isa 58:4 (wicked), Jer 4:14 (wicked), Eze 11:2 (wicked); and 6 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)
- **Observation 21503** (engagement, tag use, status draft): Wicked is here the godly, the righteous, the upright, the wicked or the ungodly as a class of persons toward whom an act is directed or whom an event befalls (the Lord keeps the godly, do not envy the wicked, Christ died for the ungodly); the act is named by another word of the verse.
  - applies to 219 occurrence(s): Gen 18:23 (wicked), Gen 18:25 (wicked), Exo 23:1 (wicked), Exo 23:7 (wicked), Num 16:26 (wicked), 1Sa 2:9 (wicked), 1Sa 24:13 (wicked), 1Sa 30:22 (wicked), 2Sa 3:34 (wicked), 2Sa 4:11 (wicked), 2Ch 19:2 (wicked), Job 3:17 (wicked); and 207 more (sheet `Observation nodes`)

### 4.2 Other recorded observations (388)

Observations of the stages that carry a written finding (prior-capture, reading, strand-reading and the like), each with the occurrences it applies to.

- **Observation 12574** (prior-capture, tag face, status draft): Face D, fearing God as a way of life: fear, fears, feared, fearing, reverence, God-fearing and worship; not a passing state but a standing, commanded, learned, taught, given, lived, joined with walking, serving, keeping and turning from evil.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Job 1:1 (evil)
- **Observation 12581** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): "Do not fear" nearly always comes with a ground, introduced by "for": "for I am with you" (Gen 26:24; Isa 41:10; 43:5; Jer 1:8; Jer 42:11; Deu 20:1; Deu 31:8); "I have given him into your hand" (Num 21:34; Deu 3:2; Jos 8:1; 10:8; 11:6); "I have redeemed you" (Isa 43:1); "I am your shield" (Gen 15:1); "God has heard the voice of the boy" (Gen 21:17); "you have borne a son" (Gen 35:17; 1Sa 4:20); "I will show you kindness" (2Sa 9:7); "those who are with us are more than those who are with them" (2Ki 6:16); idols "cannot do evil" (Jer 10:5); "am I in the place of God? ... I will provide for you" (Gen 50:19, 21); "for God has come to test you" (Exo 20:20).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 10:5 (evil)
- **Observation 12587** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): The root is rendered as a manner of acting: "One who is wise is cautious and turns away from evil, but a fool is reckless" (Pro 14:16); "he who shuns an oath" (Ecc 9:2); "he who reveres the commandment will be rewarded" (Pro 13:13); among Pharaoh's servants "whoever feared the word of the Lord ... hurried his slaves and his livestock into the houses" (Exo 9:20).
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 14:16 (evil), Ecc 9:2 (evil)
- **Observation 12598** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The fear of God, named by what it is: "The fear of the Lord is the beginning of knowledge"; "The fear of the Lord is hatred of evil"; "a fountain of life"; "It was a covenant of fear, and he feared me"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 8:13 (evil)
- **Observation 12643** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Who meets it: one event, two responses: "When justice is done, it is a joy to the righteous but terror to evildoers"; one scroll, the officials "turned one to another in fear", "neither the king nor any of his servants who heard all these words was afraid"; "people fainting with fear" and "straighten up and raise your heads"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 21:15 (evildoers)
- **Observation 12645** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): One's own conduct: "rulers are not a terror to good conduct, but to bad"; "if you do wrong, be afraid"; "respect to whom respect is owed"; "I also will choose harsh treatment for them and bring their fears upon them, because when I called, no one answered"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 13:4 (wrong)
- **Observation 12663** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Fear leads to kept from sin, turning from evil: "that the fear of him may be before you, that you may not sin"; "by the fear of the Lord one turns away from evil"
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 16:6 (evil)
- **Observation 12683** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Fear and Wickedness (M58); righteousness (M12): Fear of God joined with turning from evil; justice "a joy to the righteous but terror to evildoers" (verses shared or near: 44 / 22; 52 / 32).
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 16:6 (evil), Pro 21:15 (evildoers)
- **Observation 12783** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Ecc 12:1-7 is one figure of old age, framed by "Remember also your Creator in the days of your youth, before the evil days come" (12:1) and ending "the dust returns to the earth ... and the spirit returns to God who gave it" (12:7).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Ecc 12:1 (evil)
- **Observation 12882** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Quick temper and evil devices, side by side.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 14:17 (evil devices)
- **Observation 12895** (prior-capture, tag face, status draft): Face of aph (H0639), The face lifted in pride: The face/nose raised (Psa 10:4 ("In the pride of his face the wicked does not seek him")). Pride is the nose held high (S, lexical).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 10:4 (wicked)
- **Observation 12901** (prior-capture, tag term, status draft): charah (H2734): anger kindled · "fret" = heat yourself (Gen 4:5; Jon 4:1, 4, 9; Psa 37:1, 7, 8; Pro 24:19). Turned on another, it burns outward. Turned inward at the wicked's success, it "tends only to evil" (S).
  - applies to 3 occurrence(s): Psa 37:7 (evil), Psa 37:8 (evil), Pro 24:19 (wicked)
- **Observation 12953** (prior-capture, tag face, status draft): Form of anger, On the false prophets and the wicked (the storm) (5 / 4 hits and verses; faces 2B; units 2).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jer 23:19 (wicked)
- **Observation 13040** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): A person's anger leads to folly, evil, a snare: "A man of quick temper acts foolishly"; "Fret not yourself; it tends only to evil"; "lest you learn his ways and entangle yourself in a snare".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 37:8 (evil)
- **Observation 13072** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): A person responds to God's anger by put off what the wrath comes on: "But now you must put them all away: anger, wrath, malice, slander, and obscene talk from your mouth" (Col 3:8).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Col 3:8 (malice)
- **Observation 13082** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The humbling that turned the wrath, and the heart still not set: "And he did evil, for he did not set his heart to seek the Lord".
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): 2Ch 12:14 (evil)
- **Observation 13154** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Sequence in Rom 12:17–21: repay no evil → live peaceably → never avenge yourselves: leave it to the wrath of God → feed the enemy → overcome evil with good.
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Rom 12:17 (evil), Rom 12:21 (evil)
- **Observation 13161** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Anger and Wickedness (M58); sin (M56): The cause named before the anger: "because of the evil of your deeds"; "Fret not yourself; it tends only to evil"; "one given to anger causes much transgression".
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Psa 37:8 (evil), Jer 4:4 (evil)
- **Observation 13168** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Anger and Malice and enmity (M06): Hatred named as the source of anger and envy; Job reads the anger as being hated; malice beside anger in the lists.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Eph 4:31 (malice)
- **Observation 13187** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): Anger and Wickedness (M58), side by side with fear: fear side, fear of God joined with turning from evil (Pro 16:6); anger side, the cause named before the anger (Jer 4:4).
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 16:6 (evil), Jer 4:4 (evil)
- **Observation 13202** (prior-capture, tag link, status draft): What a person's anger leads to: heard → anger → broke the tablets (Exo 32:19); angry → put them in custody (Gen 40:2–3); company of the angry → learning his ways → a snare (Pro 22:24–25); fret → tends only to evil (Psa 37:8); angry → afraid (2Sa 6:8–9); angry → cried to the Lord all night (1Sa 15:11); Naboth's word → vexed and sullen → would eat no food (1Ki 21:4); provoke your children → lest they become discouraged (Col 3:21).

  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 37:8 (evil)

- **Observation 15499** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Under the unjust. "Servants, be subject to your masters with all respect, not only to the good and gentle but also to the unjust. For this is a gracious thing, when, mindful of God, one endures sorrows while suffering unjustly … because Christ also suffered for you, leaving you an example" (1Pe 2:18–21): Submission here is bearing wrong, with God in mind and Christ as the pattern; Compare Gen 16:9 (batch 1): Hagar told to return and submit to a mistress who had dealt harshly, with "the Lord has listened to your affliction".

- **Observation 15531** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): "No other M-code" is a fact about the single verse, not about what it says. In almost every case the inner act sits in the verses next to it. Examples: the proud in Jam 4:6, partiality and "evil thoughts" in Jam 2:4, "very sad" in Luk 18:23, "received him joyfully" in Luk 19:6, and "indignantly" in Mar 14:4. Read in their passages, the 27 fall into six groups.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 2:4 (evil)
- **Observation 15545** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Jam 2:3 ("You stand over there"): The act: partiality. A gold ring and fine clothing are seated, shabby clothing is sent to stand (2:2–3); What James calls it: "have you not then made distinctions among yourselves and become judges with evil thoughts?" (2:4); Against it: "has not God chosen those who are poor in the world to be rich in faith" (2:5), and "you have dishonored the poor man" (2:6); The poor man is the party acted on. The inner act, judging with evil thoughts, is in those who seat him; 2:4–6 are cited in 10.7, 10.10, Ch 11 and 13.1. 2:3 is not.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 2:4 (evil)
- **Observation 15700** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): God's own repeated self-description as one who does no injustice (Zeph 3:5, Deut 32:4, Ps 92:15) is the fixed standard the reversal of the righteous person (Ezekiel's formula) is measured against.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 92:15 (unrighteousness)
- **Observation 15703** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): H5753A/B (avah) makes the metaphor's two halves visible in one root split into two codes: the physical sense (bent, bowed down in pain and anguish, Isa 21:3, Ps 38:6) and the moral sense (the confession formula we have sinned and acted perversely, 1 Kgs 8:47, 2 Chr 6:37, Dan 9:5) are literally the same Hebrew word, split only for cataloguing.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Dan 9:5 (wrong)
- **Observation 15708** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): H2555 (chamas, violence, 43 verses) is the family's real anchor, structural and social and not incidental: it defines violence as a filled-world condition rather than isolated acts. The paradigm case is the pre-Flood indictment, the earth was filled with violence (Gen 6:11, 6:13); the recurring pattern afterward is social and structural: false witness (Ps 27:12, Exod 23:1), dishonest commerce and oppression of the poor (Ps 12:5, Amos 3:10), and a garment of violence worn by the wicked as a visible attribute (Ps 73:6, Ezek 7:23).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Exo 23:1 (malicious)
- **Observation 15713** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): One violation voids the whole standard: the same argument recurs from the guilt subgroup. G3848's Jas 2:11 (if you do not commit adultery but do murder, you have become a transgressor of the law) is the identical argument already found under G1777 in the guilt subgroup (Jas 2:10, become guilty of all of it): the same author applying the same whole-law-is-one-unit logic to two different M10 gloss-families independently.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Jam 2:11 (transgressor)
- **Observation 15718** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): H6586 (pasha) fuses transgress and rebel as one verb, and its verses run political revolt (Moab against Israel, Edom against Judah, 2 Kgs 1:1, 3:5, 3:7, 8:22) alongside straightforward religious confession (Lam 3:42, Ps 51:13) without any separation: the same word describes a vassal state revolting from its sovereign and a person confessing sin against God, suggesting the underlying figure is sovereignty and rebellion against a ruler, applied to both political and theological registers alike.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Psa 51:13 (transgressors)
- **Observation 15729** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): G0646, G0763 and G0764 (Greek) shift register slightly: less about a specific broken bond, more a general orientation of impiety opposed to godliness as a category (Rom 1:18 pairs ungodliness with unrighteousness; Titus 2:12 opposes it to living self-controlled, upright and godly).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:18 (unrighteousness)
- **Observation 15745** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Cruelty is defined by the absence of mercy, stated explicitly, not implied. Three of H0394's six verses use the near-identical formula cruel and have no mercy (Jer 6:23, 50:42) or merciless (Jer 30:14); Prov 12:10 makes the same point as a paradox, the mercy of the wicked is cruel, that is, even their gentlest disposition still qualifies as cruelty. Cruelty here is not a separate vice alongside mercilessness; the texts treat the two as the same fact stated twice.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 12:10 (wicked)
- **Observation 15784** (prior-capture, tag use, status draft): asebeia (G0763) as a noun in ordinary case variation across most occurrences: Rom 1:18's opening indictment (ungodliness and unrighteousness), the pastoral-epistle warnings (2 Tim 2:16's more and more ungodliness, Titus 2:12's renunciation), and Rom 11:26's eschatological promise (banish ungodliness from Jacob).
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Rom 1:18 (unrighteousness)
- **Observation 15826** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): A heart that cannot be quiet: the wicked are like the tossing sea, for it cannot be quiet, and its waters toss up mire and dirt; there is no peace, says my God, for the wicked (Isa 57:20-21). The book already has the arrogant man never at rest (Hab 2:5) and the quieted soul of Psalm 131, but not this picture.

- **Observation 17307** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 3. (a) Why the verses were not narrated: There is one main reason. The M47 reset narrated the seat words (heart, soul, spirit, flesh) and left the desire side of each verse to a later strand. This is that strand. So 10.6 *Wanting* was short (38 lines). It had nothing on: desire in the heart through the eye (Mat 5:28; Pro 6:25); delight and its objects (Psa 119:70; Isa 55:2; Pro 18:2; Psa 40:14); the wilderness craving (Num 11); evil desire (Pro 21:10; Psa 10:3; Mic 7:3); longing for people (1Th 2:17; Rom 1:11); what is desired being taken away (Eze 24:21, 25); Ch 5 had no account of a life held precious (1Sa 26:21; 2Ki 1:13–14; Pro 6:26; Psa 49:7–8).; Two smaller reasons:
  - applies to 2 occurrence(s): Pro 21:10 (evil), Mic 7:3 (evil)

- **Observation 20489** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): 9.2 What thoughts are: Design: forming something that does not yet exist. H2803G (19 rows) describes craftsmanship: cherubim "skillfully worked" (Exod 26:1, 31) and "skilled design" (Exod 39:3). The faculty itself is neutral. *Maḥăšāvāh* covers both artistry and plots, and *mᵊzimmāh* covers both "discretion" (Prov 2:11) and "evil devices" (Prov 12:2). What makes a thought good or evil is what it is designed toward.; Reckoning: giving something its value or category. - **Observation 20491** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Where responsibility sits. The commands address the stages from lodging onward: "forsake … his thoughts" (Isa 55:7); "let none of you devise evil" (Zech 7:10); "take every thought captive" (2 Cor 10:5); "wash your heart" (Jer 4:14); The *arising* is not commanded, but it is not neutral either. What comes up shows the condition of the heart (Gen 6:5; Mark 7:21). So thoughts that arise reveal the heart, and devising engages the will. The heart itself can be changed from outside: written on, given discretion, guarded, or darkened.
  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Zec 7:10 (evil)

## 5. Claims and the narrative

77 claims rest on these occurrences: 1 on an observation of these words and 76 reached only because one of their sources is a verse where a member word stands. The second group is about its verse, not about the collection; it is kept so that nothing stored is hidden, and labelled.

### 5.1 Claims resting on an observation of these words (1)

- **C-365** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Devising is also design and skill, given by God; its direction makes it good or evil, toward God's house, a king's strength or one's own ease.
  - beyond the text: reads design as one skill whose use changes its character
  - rests on: observation 12414 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Pro 12:2)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 0 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
